ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u4	Thank you very much. Mr Ryszard Bartosik, Minister of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, will reply. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u5	Dear Marshal! High Seym! First, a few words about the regulation. A Regulation of the Council of Ministers setting out the conditions for granting aid to agricultural producers who, both in 2019 and in 2020, have not received at least once a payment for the milk sold in connection with the issuing of an order declaring bankruptcy against that person to whom the milk has been sold, is a response to the requests made by farmers, their associations and farmers' organisations in this respect. Pursuant to the provisions of this Regulation, the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture grants aid to milk producers up to the level of, first, the amount of lost income on the sale of milk and at the same time 80% the amount resulting from unpaid invoices, bills or other evidence of sale. This aid shall be granted in the form of de minimis aid. Milk producers could submit applications for the aid until 15 August this year. The request was accompanied by a copy of the invoices, accounts or other evidence of the sale of milk, a copy of the list of claims or any other document confirming the existence of a claim by the agricultural producer against the fallen entity for not receiving payment by that producer for the sold milk in 2019 and 2020, drawn up by the insolvency administrator, by the co-holder's written consent. The aid was allocated to the amount of PLN 10 million. According to data from the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture, milk producers submitted 786 applications for this aid. The agency's county offices are currently carrying out a process of verifying the applications submitted. At 14 September 755 applications for a total amount of over PLN 7 million were verified, but this verification is still ongoing. The remaining applications are being completed by agricultural producers due to the need to complete the required documents which confirm the lost income in the years in question, i.e. in 2019 and 2020. The applications were submitted mainly by the suppliers of the cooperative "Bielmlek" and Ozorków. High Izbo! The regulation responds to the difficult situation of farmers supplying milk to dairy cooperatives who have not paid farmers the funds for the raw material delivered. The regulation concerned all dairy cooperatives which had not paid the farmers funds in the years 2019 to 2020, and indeed this Regulation does not cover the situation in which milk farmers found themselves and who did not receive payment for the milk delivered due to the bankruptcy of the dairy cooperative in Rypin, as this happened in 2018. We had 10 million zlotys for this purpose in connection with the situation which you spoke about in the question. We will examine this regulation at the Ministry of Agriculture. However, I must also say that when it comes to the loss of income from other supplies, I think it will be very difficult And it's gonna have to be regulated on a system basis. Such work continues, e.g. in the case of the Family Farm Act, within the framework of the agricultural legislation in the Polish Lad programme. We want to settle this, but the matter is very delicate. The state, the Ministry of Agriculture, wants to help in such difficult situations, but the scale of the problem is so large that system regulations are needed in this area. And as far as this particular situation is concerned, we are going to analyse it, because we are helping farmers according to the question asked and we are helping farmers according to the facts. [[♪ The bell ♪]] the provisions of the Regulation. We help those whose dairy cooperatives have fallen in the years 2019 and 2020. Thank you very much.

By September 12 we recorded 2260 attempts to cross the Belarusian-Polish border illegally. Only yesterday - I received information from the Border Guard this morning - 301 migrants tried to cross the border. The Polish state took the necessary and appropriate steps to seal the Polish-Belarusian border. Let me remind you that we had a readmission agreement, i.e. refugees, people illegally crossing the border were to be accepted back by Belarus. At the end of June, the procedure for expressing this agreement on the part of Belarus was launched. This is evidence of a certain conscious operation of the service. Besides, there's a lot more to this evidence, of course. The main purpose of the operation of the guard supported by the Polish Army, supported by the Police, is to seal the border, i.e. to prevent the crossing, but from 1 January to 12 September the Border Guard stopped 1481 people who illegally entered the Polish state from Belarus. These devices, of course, make it very difficult to cross the border, in principle without the help of the Belarusian services, these activities are impossible for these migrants. I wanted to stress that we are prepared for further action. I am not authorized to talk about the government's plan for the rest of the year or for the years to come, but we will definitely spend money on it. In the previous year, also the decision, first the government, because it is a government initiative and later the Sejm, the Senate and the President, we decided to extend the operation of the Government Road Development Fund and allocate PLN 2 billion to finance the construction of bypasses in the lines of the voivodeship roads. As regards the recruitment carried out by the Ministry of Infrastructure, the call for funding for the construction of bypasses in voivodeship roads was announced on 21 April 2021 and on 21 May was the last day for submission of applications. However, according to the Act, I stress, according to the Act, the Ministry of Infrastructure has 3 months from the end of recruitment for the transmission of the letter for approval by the President of the Council of Ministers. We sent these letters to the Chancellery of the Prime Minister at the end of July this year, so that before this statutory deadline. The second stage of the selection is the examination and approval of the letter by the President of the Council of Ministers. This procedure follows directly from the law which set up the Government Road Development Fund, and we are obliged to follow this procedure. So that's the way it is right now. I would add that the President of the Council of Ministers is not bound by any time limit for taking his decision. The Act does not order the decision and approval of the letter by the President of the Council of Ministers within a time limit, I do not know, 1, 2 or 3 months, there is no such provision, therefore the Prime Minister has any time to approve these letters. Such decisions cannot be made on an ad hoc basis. Firstly, these are very large funds, I stress, 2 billion PLN. We have seen many such places that need improvement, hence the decision of the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki to create this programme and, above all, to secure funds for this purpose. Almost 28 billion PLN - this will cost us this investment. Here I want to thank Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, but also the Ministry of Finance - Minister Piotr Patkowski is here with us. Cooperation in this area is really very good, the Ministry of Finance does not save money for road investments, so we can implement them. As far as the programme itself is concerned, it was announced by Minister Andrzej Adamczyk in February 2020, then those investments which are on the basic list and are currently in progress were identified. This is 100 road tasks. These investments are carried out throughout Poland. We did not want to exclude any region from this program, therefore the larger voivodships such as the Mazowieckie voivodship or the Wielkopolskie voivodship, and smaller voivodships: Opolskie, Świętokrzyskie are included in this program. At this moment 13 contracts for the construction of bypasses were signed, at the tender stage there is one investment - Strykowa bypass on the national road no. 32 and yet this year we are planning to announce another two tendering procedures for the construction of the bypass of Gorayc and Koźmin Wielkopolski bypass on the national road No. 15. So that this process is progressing very strongly and is consistently implemented by the Ministry of Infrastructure. As far as the Kalisz ring road is concerned, I must admit that Kalisz is such a natural beneficiary of this programme, as it is probably the most heavily burdened city in Poland. The average daily traffic burden is up to 25,000. vehicles within one 24-hour period. It's really a lot, and it's surprising that there's been no government initiative to build this city's ring road before. And I very much thank the Members of this district, Mr Jan Mosiński, Mr Piotr Kaleta, for signaling us this problem, for stressing that this issue needs to be resolved, that it is simply Kalisz's ring road that needs to be implemented. Work is currently underway on the multi-criterial analysis of the existing three variants of the Kalisz ring road construction along the national road No 25. The work carried out by the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways seeks to identify the best, i.e. the optimal option. All options are assessed on the basis of this analysis. The choice of the best option on individual sections does not preclude the creation of a result option, which may arise from a combination of different options, of course if this is practically possible. The final option of the Kalisz ring road will be identified on the basis of a multi-criterial analysis which contains environmental, social, technical and economic aspects. Thanks to the analysis developed, and it is at the final stage at the moment, it will be possible to decide on the option recommended for the application for an environmental decision. The selected Kalisz bypass will be indicated in the application for an environmental decision as a preferred option. According to our schedule, this is to take place in the 2nd quarter of next year, i.e. in the 2nd quarter of next year the General Directorate of National Roads and Motorways Branch in Poznań will submit a request for an environmental decision on the Kalisz ring road. Then, after obtaining such a decision (the bell), we will have to tender for the programme concept, which is to clarify from the technical point of view the course of this route. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u73	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to this question, it is worth to start with why rating agencies are important to us. These are completely independent, not only from national countries, but also from the European Commission, bodies that not only assess the economic stability of countries in general, but also penetrate budget forecasts, forecasts of GDP growth or inflation. It is worth noting that, of course, the overall assessment itself is important for us, but Fitch, in addition to forecasting GDP growth this year and in the following years, also refers to the projected deficit of the entire sector in this and the following year. Why is this important? This is important in the context of the allegations that the government allegedly hides public expenditure, that outside the so-called. Official deficits are being pushed out streams of money that you can't see anywhere. Fitch's reviews, but also other credit rating agencies, are lying about it. If we look at what Fitch says in his official communications, we will see that he provides very clear and precise data on the deficit of the entire sector, i.e. all expenditure incurred by the public sector in Poland, including PFR, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego fund and any other funds. Since the government itself in the explanatory memorandum of the Budget Act gives the amount of this deficit and the credit rating agencies give the amount of this deficit, then it is not possible to talk about the government's concealment of any debt. If that's official data, you can't talk about secrecy. Turning to the details of what Fitch has shown, it is worth noting that when it comes to debt ratings in foreign currencies in Poland, the long-term assessment remains unchanged. That's the grade. And? The short-term assessment for foreign currencies was raised to F1 from F2. In the case of the national currency, it remained unchanged; is it A? and short-term F1. As regards the detailed projections for macroeconomic indicators for the Polish economy, the forecast for economic growth for this year is 5.2%. That's more than we expect as a Polish government, because we anticipate 4.9. Next year... And this is an increase from 4.4, which is Fitch itself raising the growth forecast for this year. 4.5% will be in 2022 and 3.8% - in 2023. With regard to the inflation forecast, which is the most recent indicator of interest, this is 3.9% next year. , and for 2023, 3,6%. So Fitch shows that in 2023, we're going to start going back to the maximum inflation target, but still set by the NBP. This is also very important information. As far as the general government deficit forecast is concerned, I will stress once again, taking into account all public expenditure, that is 5% for 2021. That's less than the Polish government expects. This is due to the fact that Fitch does not foresee the full use of anti-crisis tools, i.e. debt from the Anti-Action Fund COVID-19 and PFR - 3.8% in 2022 and 3% in 2023. So, according to Fitch in 2023, we will now return to the full consolidation of public finances required by the Maastricht Treaty. As far as government debt is concerned, which I will emphasise again, the entire debt, including all national funds, is expected to be 57,5% by 2020 in 2023,55.3%. We are still below the maximum limit of debt set by the Constitution. It is also worth noting that in its forecasts Fitch did not take into account the effects of the introduction of the Polish Load program, a program which may further raise our macroeconomic indicators - i.e. we still have a reserve for possible growth. When it comes to showing macroeconomic indicators of Poland against other countries, it is worth to point out here that Poland, as I said at the beginning, remains unchanged outside the ranking of foreign currencies for short-term debt. On the other hand, the credit assessment for the European Union has changed 19 times, including five times from stable to negative. A change in the credit assessment occurred in four countries. Fitch increased the credit assessment for Greece and Lithuania and reduced it for Slovakia and Italy. Now I'm going to give examples of a few countries. For Austria, the outlook has changed from positive to stable, i.e. the assessment has been reduced. For Belgium, it's stable to negative. For Croatia and Cyprus - with positive for stable. For Finland - with positive for stable. For France, it's stable to negative. Greece, as I have already mentioned, has an assessment raised from stable to positive. Lithuania - with positive for stable. Latvia - stable to negative. Malta - with positive for stable. Portugal - with positive for stable. Romania - stable to negative. Italy - from negative to stable, which means here is an increase. This shows, however, that most countries have a change of perspective to be worse, which, given the scale of the current crisis, is still not a very devastating change for these economies. However, Poland is on the plus side, the more that this perspective remains the same for us (the bell), i.e. it has not been reduced, and secondly, in one element it has been increased yet. Thank you. [[Applause]]

This gives absolute certainty and stability that the tax revenue then established will be very similar to what we assumed... Or positive. The second importance such credit rating agencies have is the impact on the profitability of fiscal securities issued by Poland in order to cover debt. The more positive the rating of Poland, but also the closer macro-economic readings to those presented by the Polish government, the more certainty investors are when it comes to investing in these bonds. This is why we can talk today about the record low profitability of fiscal securities issued by Poland. Because this is a guarantee that Poland is a macroeconomically stable country to invest, it is a certainty that Poland is also a country where profits from its business activity can be actively predicted. This is also some of the reasons that are important to us when it comes to credit rating agencies, but the most important thing is that, contrary to what is said, the government hides public expenditure, that the government misconstrues macroeconomic indicators, it is precisely the ratings of the European Commission's credit rating agencies that are clearly lying about it, because they are really very similar to what is provided for in the budget law. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u165	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! For years, the health sector has needed to increase its financial resources. Following the 2015 parliamentary elections, the Law and Justice Government gradually and dynamically increased its financial expenditure for health care in Poland. In 2015, these expenditures amounted to PLN 77.1 billion, which was at that time 4,7% GDP. For 2021, the government has planned health expenditure of over PLN 140 billion, i.e. 6.17% GDP. Health expenditure is expected to reach 7% in 2027. GDP. These very large financial expenditures were accompanied by large increases in health workers' salaries. Doctors with the second degree of specialisation achieved an increase in wages from around PLN 8000 to over PLN 13 600 in 2020, which represents an increase of 70% in 2015. Doctors with the first degree of specialization received a salary increase during this period from 6800 PLN to 10 800 PLN, i.e. 60% increase. Doctors without specialization received an increase of 64%, from PLN 5200 to PLN 8600. Nurses and specialized midwives received salary increases from PLN 3800 up to PLN 6570, i.e. 69% increase. The other nurses received an increase in salary from PLN 3400 to PLN 6130, which represents an increase of 80%. In this period, rescuers also received an increase in wages from PLN 2800 to PLN 4077. This level of increase in financial expenditure on health protection has never taken place before.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u166	We proceed with the examination of item 14 of the agenda: Report of the Infrastructure Commission on the Government draft law amending the Road Transport Act and some other laws (Prints No 1508 and 1543). I ask Mr Jerzy Paul to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u167	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Infrastructure on the Government's draft law amending the Road Transport Act and some other laws in print No 1508. On 14 September, the first reading of the project took place at the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure, as requested by the Marshal of the Sejm. The aim of the project is to implement Directive 2018/645 of the European Union amending Directive 2003/59/EC on the initial qualification and periodic training of drivers of certain road vehicles for the carriage of goods or passengers and Directive 2006/126/EC on driving licences. Implementation of the provisions cited above. directives only regulate the rules for professional drivers, i.e. those who already carry out road transport on a daily basis by trucks or buses. These are training and examinations carried out after obtaining a driving licence, aimed at the safe, efficient and lawful pursuit of the driver's profession of heavy vehicles. The most important changes will be to digitise the circulation of documents in the process of training professional drivers and improve the quality of training of professional drivers. The new rules will ensure that training is properly conducted without the possibility of their illegal shortening or forging a certificate of completion of training, as well as eliminating paper medical and psychological certificates for electronic entry. Then, allowing part of the training for professional drivers through e-learning will speed up the implementation of training and to some extent allow flexibility for both professional drivers and trainers. By amending the rules, a driver qualification card will be introduced allowing for easier employment of professional drivers from a third country, ensuring that all training and research necessary for the safe execution of road transport will be carried out and confirmed in Poland on the basis of Polish documents. The next amendment will concern initial qualification exams for professional drivers, which will be transferred from the regional offices to the WORDs. This will reduce the costs of exams and increase accessibility for citizens. After the amendment of the regulations, cooperation between the voivode, the Road Transport Inspection and the State Labour Inspection will allow to check whether the driver actually completed the training, whether he was at that time, for example. somewhere on the road. Thanks to the amendment of the Act, the possibility of verifying data concerning Polish and foreign drivers will be introduced during the inspection by the relevant services. This provision will improve the detection of persons who drive vehicles despite a ban or who do not have the right to drive. This provision will apply to Polish and foreign drivers. The new regulations will give the possibility of regaining the Polish driving licence by persons who have withdrawn their driving licence abroad. Drivers will be treated equally with others who have withdrawn Polish driving rights and will have to meet the same conditions in order to recover them. The Act abolishes the regionalization of medical and psychological examinations in voivodship occupational medicine centres for drivers. This provision will contribute to the fact that persons who are to be tested at a voivodship labour medicine centre will be entitled to choose any centre that is able to carry out the tests as soon as possible. Another change will be for drivers driving under the influence of alcohol. After stopping a driving licence under the law they will be required to undergo a medical and psychological examination and conduct a training course. Changes will also apply to persons who will be suspended and who will be entitled to drive for that period. This will allow them to draw consequences for driving during the detention of a driving licence, as for persons without a licence. High Izbo! The Law and Justice Government has been working for many years to increase safety on Polish roads, introducing new regulations and some adapting to the requirements of EU law. The bill we are working on is the best example of this. High Seym! The Committee on Infrastructure supports the abovementioned. amendments to the draft law and asks the High House to adopt them. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u168	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. I'm opening a discussion. Jerzy Paul, Law and Justice, will be the first voice in the debate. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u169	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! According to these proposals, the provisions of the above-mentioned legislation have been implemented. directives only regulate the rules for professional drivers, i.e. those who carry out road transport on a daily basis by trucks or buses. These are training and examinations carried out after obtaining a driving licence, aimed at the safe and lawful pursuit of the driver's profession of heavy vehicles. The changes concern: improving the quality of training of professional drivers - persons intending to carry out or carrying out road transport on the driver's post, e-learning training method, introducing a driver qualification card, digitising the circulation of documents, initial qualification tests, increasing supervision of the training of professional drivers, introducing the possibility of regaining the Polish driving licence for drivers from abroad, abolishing the division of medical and psychological examinations, procedures applied to drivers under the influence of alcohol and suspension of rights. The Law and Justice Club, given that the proposed amendments to the Act are an adaptation to EU law and have been positively accepted by the Infrastructure Commission, will vote in favour of the bill. This amendment concerns the simplification of the procedure by removing the consent and the requirements necessary to obtain it for distance learning using computer and Internet techniques. For conducting classes in WW. form, i.e. e-learning, no consent will be needed, no decision will be taken. Only a 3-year traineeship will be required for the running of a training centre in road transport. Each facility will be able to run e-learning once this condition has been met. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u172	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Maciej Kopiec, the Left Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u173	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft law amending the Road Transport Act and some other laws amends the Road Transport Act. It is intended to implement European Union law. The laws on road traffic, the service of occupational medicine, the drivers of vehicles, the Penal Code and public documents are also being amended. The project concerns the transposition into Polish legislation of Directive 2018/645 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 April 2018 and Directive 2006/126EC on driving licences, amendments to, inter alia. training of persons who intend to or carry out road transport in the driver's post, supervision of training centres and adaptation of the circulation of documents and training data to the needs of the proposed changes. The project introduces a professional driver profile, i.e. the obligation for a driver candidate to have a profile containing a set of data identifying the applicant for entry of code 95 confirming the issue of the professional qualification certificate, the driver qualification card and the performance of medical examinations. The profile is also to include updated information on whether the allowances have not been revoked. The Act introduces the digitalisation of the circulation of professional drivers' documents and the elimination of medical certificates in paper form. This draft law also provides for changes in the training of professional drivers by defining rules for the operation of e-learning and the possibility for training companies to conduct such training. The project moves exams from the regional offices to the WORDs, which according to the government's commitment is to increase availability and speed up the acquisition of powers. It also empowers WORDs to issue professional qualifications certificates, which are also to be issued in digital form, entered in the central register of drivers and the professional driver profile. A new document appears, i.e. a driver qualification card issued to persons who cannot issue a Polish driver's license with code 95 in it. The rules implemented are intended to give the police the opportunity to fully verify the data during the driver checks. The Act abolishes the regionalization of occupational medicine centers. The driver may choose a centre which has free time limits to speed up the authorisation procedure. The Act enhances the control of drivers' training centres and introduces the possibility of withdrawing driver privileges which have been trained at a training centre which has not conducted the training in accordance with the required time or has not had adequate training powers. We support the opposition amendments tabled at the committee meeting by Mr Suchon, which say that drivers should not be punished by immediately withdrawing their powers and give them time to complete the missing training and documents. We also support the amendment which removes the obligation to own a vehicle from training entities in the e-learning system. Unfortunately, when observing the work in the Infrastructure Committee, it should be noted that many issues that are raised by practitioners, businesses that are also covered by the Act are not taken into account. Adapting the right to reality would be very beneficial, but the project is unfortunately processed quickly, while the content of European Union law has been known to the government since 2018. There are little points that could really help implement a good project because the government's position is in advance negative for some of the social comments. I hope and hope that this process will improve in committee. The Left Club will support this project in keeping with the above reservations. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u197	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the draft law amending the Act on Trade Reduction on Sundays and holidays and some other days, print No 1446. According to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm Marshals of the Sejm addressed on 3 August 2021 the draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for first reading and examination. The Act of 10 January 2018 on the restriction of trade on Sundays and holidays and on some other days entered into force on 1 March 2018. The main purpose of the Act was to gradually reduce trade in such a way as to enable trade workers, mostly women and mothers, to spend Sundays with families - a day which these families also have free of work. The assessment of the impact of the trade restriction on Sundays by the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology in 2019 shows that these restrictions did not have negative effects on trade. On the contrary, there has been a development of tourist services, catering services, accommodation services, which has increased the number of beneficiaries of the positive effects of this law. This undoubtedly brought Poland closer to the Western model of life, where trade facilities on Sundays and holidays are closed for a long time. The solidarity of our fellow countrymen, who, according to the data provided by employers' organisations and trade unions, shop from Monday to Saturday, is not to be underestimated. This amendment aims to eliminate the problem of circumvention of trade bans by certain establishments through the use of the exemptions contained in the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 point 7 of the Act, i.e. possession of post office status. At the same time, the current amendment responds to the expectations of entrepreneurs, extending the possibility of receiving free assistance from a spouse, own, adopted children, parents, stepmothers or stepfathers. There are also additional exemptions here, among others. for fishing holdings, agricultural products buying-in centres, and during the period from 1 June to 30 September, for establishments operating exclusively, with the emphasis on the word "only', trade in agricultural machinery or spare parts for such machinery. The proposed amendment will undoubtedly restore the principle of fair competition expected above all by businesses complying with the statutory restriction of trade. It will remove the current ambiguities and doubts regarding the application of the provisions of this law. September 14th this year the committee after a very thorough and long discussion, during which both MEPs and trade union representatives as well as employers presented their positions, carried out the first reading and considered the project. According to print report No 1453 the committee asks the High Sejm to adopt the bill submitted. I would add that the proposal was adopted with one abstention and no votes against. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u295	Mr. Marshal! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the Joint Commission: Administration and Home Affairs and Infrastructure to submit a report on the work on the government draft law amending the Law - Aviation Law and the Law on Border Guard, prints No 1448 and 1449. The regulation we are dealing with today is related to the application of the European Commission's implementing regulation from 31 December 2021. The Regulation is of November 2015 and concerns specific measures for the implementation of the common basic standards on aviation security and was amended by the Implementing Regulation on 23 January 2019. I will refer here to some general issues and make some comments on, in particular, the issues raised in the discussion on the project. Firstly, this amended regulation instead of the previous background check and pre-employment checks of persons performing certain activities in the air transport sector introduces a standard and extended background check. With regard to the change in Aviation Law, the project shall in particular specify the scope of the activities undertaken as part of a standard and extended background check, the categories of persons subject to such verification, the entities responsible for carrying out past checks and the frequency of such checks. If we refer to the whole regulation submitted by the government, it will result in its adoption and implementation into national law in order to adapt to the new European Union legislation, which is amended in order to harmonise and simplify aviation security measures, to improve the transparency of the law, to harmonise the interpretation of the rules and, above all, to ensure the most effective implementation of the common standards for civil aviation security for the European Union. With regard to these issues that have been addressed in the discussion during the work, I would first of all like to refer to the allegation that certain provisions of this regulation are not precise as regards the verification of past information from criminal records. I would like to point out that, in view of the fact that the subject-matter of the investigation is not able to obtain information from criminal records on the person to whom such verification is carried out, in the draft national legislation, i.e. the law which is the subject of today's proceedings, it has been decided that the verification in this respect is to be carried out on the basis of information obtained from the person checked, with no information being obtained from the National Criminal Register in the case of an extended background check, as in this respect the Border Guard is carried out within the framework of the findings of the existence of the so-called. negative indications. Secondly, there were questions in the committee on the extension of the catalogue of persons to be checked and the frequency of checks in the context of the regulatory burden of the checkers. I would like to draw attention to the fact that the draft identifies categories of persons subject to standard background checks, most of which are subject to a background check, also in line with existing legislation. In relation to the so-called. a standard background check of the minimum scope required by European Union legislation has been extended in the proposed regulation to persons employed at commercial or service points located in publicly accessible parts of aerodromes, or to persons performing obligations in that zone related to the operation of the aerodrome. This is due to the obvious need to strengthen the protection of public areas of airports which are particularly vulnerable to acts of unlawful interference. I do not need to explain the reasons behind the introduction of those provisions which clarify the procedures used so far on a European scale and not only. The new solution is to include an extended background check of the entire professional group of air traffic controllers, which results from the key role that controllers play in civil aviation and their access to key air traffic systems. In addition, the project, as I said at the beginning, provides for the implementation by the Border Guard of an IT system enabling operators to submit a request for verification of the existence of negative indications in electronic form through a dedicated application. According to this amendment, this will be implemented by the Border Guard. There were also so-called issues raised in the discussion. negative indications concerning these checks. With regard to this part of the law and this discussion in committee, I would like to remind you that the European Regulation gives Member States the freedom to determine the extent of the risks and offences to be guided by the assessment of the past of the person checked, but the two directives mentioned should be the minimum of that assessment. As regards the 2015 Regulation, as I said, at least the offences referred to in Annex II to that Directive on the use of Passenger Name Record data for the prevention of terrorist offences and in accordance with point 11 of the 2015 Regulation are considered not to have passed a positive background check if all elements of the verification have not been positively verified. The existence of such negative conditions shall be established not only if the person concerned is convicted by a final judgment for committing the offence listed in the catalogue set out in the Act, but also if the person concerned is informed of the criminal proceedings currently being conducted against that person, and where the Border Guard is not able to obtain information which is necessary for the determination, or if such information is insufficient, because in such a case the person concerned does not give assurance of reliability and consequently of the appropriate level of protection. The one-to-last issue I will briefly address is the lack of legislation that makes it possible to refer to the Border Guard's information on the existence of negative grounds. I stress that the information on the basis of which the Border Guard makes findings on the existence of negative grounds is, in principle, always objective and not discretionary - taking information about the conduct of, for example. the criminal proceedings against the person concerned, or even more so the conviction of such a person by a final sentence for crimes or the lack of sufficient information on the findings - therefore such an appeal would be pointless. In case of new circumstances, e.g. the termination of criminal proceedings or the failure of conviction, according to the draft, the verifying entity shall provide information justifying the lack of grounds for establishing the existence of negative grounds for the Border Guard and shall request a review. The financial impact of the Act in 2021-2030 is a total of 15 314 thousand, of which 9314 thousand. will be spent during the first 10 years of validity of the Act. These financial consequences relate to the costs of the Border Guard. Additional appropriations for the Border Guard are planned to be mobilised under the Internal Security Fund, which is governed by the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014. On the other hand, part 39 of the State budget will be the source of funding for the planned expenditure of the Civil Aviation Authority for the planned increase in employment and job equipment for new workers: Transport. It shall be increased by the amounts contained in Article. 6 of the proposed bill. So much for me, Marshal. If there are questions, of course we will address them in the course of the discussion. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u299	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! The draft envisages amending the provisions of the Law - Aviation Law and the Border Guard Act by introducing new standard and extended checks on the past and checks against employment of persons performing civil aviation security tasks in order to ensure the application of Regulation 2015/1998 as amended by Commission Implementing Regulation 2019/103 of 23 January 2019. As has already been said here, the proposal contains regulations on the scope of activities undertaken in the framework of the standard background check, the category of persons to be checked, the bodies responsible for carrying out the checks and the frequency of checks. In addition, the consequences of the negative outcome of the background check have been identified. So far, a background check has been repeated at least every five years. These deadlines are now reduced to 3 years in case of standard verification and up to 1 year or constant monitoring in case of extended background verification. The new solution is to include the system of checking the past, among others. all flight controllers, air traffic. The project also identifies cases where negative grounds for civil aviation security may be identified. The existence of negative grounds shall be established in the event of a threat to the defence or security of the State and public order or of a criminal proceeding currently under way against a person or conviction by a final judgment of that person. However, a detailed analysis of the proposed provisions raises some doubts. The scope of the background check shall include obtaining information from criminal records from all countries of residence from the last 5 years. The experience gained from many years of operation of this provision points to difficulties in the practical implementation of this obligation. Given that different types of criminal records exist in different countries, which are often inconsistent, airport manager or other economic operator obliged to carry out a standard background check, there is often no physical possibility of verifying documents submitted by a person recruited from other countries. These documents are subject to a free assessment of the entities and may lead to erroneous conclusions from the background check of the person concerned. In the proposed legislation, the catalogue of offences considered to be criminal offences resulting in negative conditions has also been substantially extended to the current law - Aviation Law. The catalogue of crimes affecting the process of checking the past has been included e.g. crimes against the environment, crimes against the family and care, crimes against the property. The catalogue of offences adopted goes far beyond the recommendations of the Article. 5 of the Council Framework Decision of 13 June 2002 on combating terrorism, as set out in Article 5 of the Council Framework Decision of 13 June 2002 on combating terrorism. 1 sets out the minimum catalogue of terrorist offences. The catalogue of offences adopted in the proposed legislation also goes beyond the list of terrorist offences as defined in Directive 2017/541 of the European Parliament and of the Council. It should also be noted that a significant increase in the group of persons to be checked and the frequency of past checks may not only have a negative impact on the verification system but also have a significant impact on the continuity of the entities obliged to carry out an extended background check, e.g. managing airports, registered agents or the Air Navigation Agency. The legislator also recognises the very fact that the Border Guard has taken notice of ongoing criminal proceedings against a person as an indication of the negative outcome of the extended review of the past, and the consequence is the inability of the person to perform the functions or tasks to which he or she was or was to be employed, which in turn has significant consequences in the field of labour law. At the same time, the legislator does not provide for the participation of the person to whom an extended background check is being carried out throughout the process of this verification. This, ladies and gentlemen, violates a number of rights and freedoms of a person. Such a solution in stress situations may result in a refusal to hire a person after false notification of the possibility of committing a crime. There is also great doubt about the provision: where it is not possible to obtain the information necessary to establish the existence of negative indications. This is done by the Border Guard. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The checker should not bear the negative consequences of the service failure. Mr. Marshal, may I extend a moment more? Thank you very much. The fact that Polish services cannot verify a person for reasons we do not know about should not automatically lead to a negative decision for the person being checked. High Izbo! Mr. Marshal! ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u309	Thank you kindly. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! This draft law is a transposition into the Polish legal system of Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/103 amending Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/1998 as regards clarification, harmonisation and simplification, as well as strengthening certain specific aviation security measures. Those provisions shall apply from 31 December. That Regulation has made significant changes to the provisions of Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) laying down detailed measures for the implementation of the common basic standards on civil aviation security concerning the verification of the past of persons carrying out civil aviation security tasks. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u325	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to begin by thanking you for the very substantive and relevant voices in this discussion. Several issues, doubts or issues raised in the discussion and in the questions that have been raised here, I would like to clarify. The first issue that emerged is the possible difficulty of checking criminal records from other countries where the person was staying in those other countries. I wanted to point out that this isn't about staying, this is the notion of living in a foreign country. It's not about a holiday visit or any other short trip. In the overwhelming majority of cases that we can imagine, it will probably be a stay in European countries where there is no such problem, where there is rapid direct cooperation between services. On the other hand, in extremely rare cases, if someone has actually lived in more exotic countries for a long time in the last 5 years - imagine that someone, for example. he was in Syria or Libya - we would like to see more insight into him. In general, I think that in this one statement I will be able to answer a whole range of doubts raised here - I think that these doubts arise from a certain mental calculator, according to which this procedure and the verification of credibility is treated as a type of quasi-court procedure, and the absence of such an attestation is a kind of criminal measure. That is not the case. Therefore, issues concerning the burden of proof, issues concerning remedies do not exist here. Here we are dealing with a completely different logic. Here is the burden of proof that someone is actually credible that there is no objection to him, on that person, so to speak, pregnant. There is no obligation, it is not a universal human right to work at the airport and enter the security restricted areas at the airport every day. There are really a whole range of other sectors of the economy where you can easily pursue your professional ambitions. After all, already in many branches and in many professions, much more than the occupation of an airport worker, it is necessary to present e.g. certificates of non-criminality or other such requirements. If we're gonna look for an analogy here, it's like... in the proceedings concerning access to classified information also justified doubts, the lack of explanation of any circumstances concerning the examination of a person, i.e. this burden of evidence is reversed, may result in the lack of access to classified information, and in turn there are such professions, there are jobs where, without access to classified information, it is impossible to work, but we consider that they are so specific, so sensitive that we apply such a procedure. The same is true of people who are currently working at airports, i.e. critical infrastructure facilities, where there is an increased terrorist threat. The European legislator recognized, and we make reception of these provisions to Polish law, that these people require an elevated level of trust. There's enough serious, legitimate doubt here. There is no need to prove, it is not necessary to ensure that a person is involved e.g. for some terrorist action, but it's enough to justify a serious doubt not to give such a person a certificate. Hence, for example. This wide range of crimes, not only terrorist crimes, but any serious crimes, because people who have committed serious crimes in the past may have links with organized criminal groups or criminal environments, be influenced by them, be under their pressure, may be subject to, for example. Blackmailers may not want their past to be exposed, and someone can come to them and for example. I know what you've done, and like this package I'm giving you here, you're not gonna take it to the airport, and I'm gonna tell you all about it. These people are just more vulnerable to this kind of action, so we wouldn't want them to work in these sensitive, critical places and have regular, daily access to them. Hence also the issue of remedies. It's not like there's no appeal procedure. It is written that, in the event of information about the new circumstances of the case, i.e. if someone feels that he has been wronged by such conduct, because some important, important circumstances for him have not been taken into account in the proceedings, the authority may report such a fact to the authority. If this is indeed a new circumstance, this will be repeated. Several votes were about the fact that this was supposed to be the last minute. Let's not overreact. These provisions are so defined at European level that they enter into force from 1 January 2022. For the last few months actually, preparations for their implementation are underway, carried out in parallel with the work on these provisions, so it is not so that only after they have been adopted e.g. Border Guard's gonna start doing this, preparing it, preparing it. No, it's already happening. The Border Guard is as ready as possible to carry out these new duties. A slight shift to November 15th of one of the duties. I'm already answering another question here. Yes, that is why we have made such an amendment with our approval that the original deadline of 31 October has just caused the risk that, with the maximum use of all constitutional terms by the subsequent authorities of the legislative process, this law will not be implemented earlier. On November 15th, there is no such danger. As regards costs and nuisances, the costs for people - Mr Suchoń asked - there will be no such costs, i.e. on the part of the person to be checked, there are no charges to be paid in order to be subject to such checks. So those costs won't be there. As far as nuisance is concerned, probably more for entities that operate an airport activity and would like to hire an employee, I understand that there may be a nuisance in that, for example. They won't be able to hire him right away, but they'll just have to wait for that check. Perhaps this is a nuisance, and it already exists, only to a lesser extent. On the other hand, we are trying to ensure that there are no particular delays in the extended range of persons and the directory of cases to be checked. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u331	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up at Strasbourg on 8 November 2001. This Convention is another instrument in addition to the Convention for the Protection of the Architecture Heritage and the Convention for the Protection of the Archaeological Heritage, aimed at creating certain harmonised solutions in European countries. National heritage, which the Republic of Poland is obliged to protect by virtue of art. Five constitutions, our constitutions, are extremely valuable. At present, we also understand audiovisual heritage. The purpose of the Convention is to harmonise standards for the transmission and storage of audiovisual works. The technological progress that we are witnessing on a daily basis will, as a rule, force the introduction of new legal solutions. In the Polish system, of course, we already have the relevant legal acts, such as the law on copyright and related rights. However, there is no doubt that amendments will be necessary in this regard. For in the future we will be faced with a completely different reality, probably one that we cannot imagine at this moment. Today, on the other hand, we want to be involved in the process of creating solutions to ensure consistency in legislation on the collection, protection and dissemination of film collections. All Member States of the European Union already have systems for collecting, preserving cinematographic works, which form part of their audiovisual heritage. In analysing the Convention, it can be concluded that both Polish legal acts and the practice of the functioning of cultural institutions meet European standards, hence there is no need to change the current regulations in Poland at the moment. In conclusion, the Republic of Poland should be involved in such activities of the Council of Europe in order to jointly build and implement solutions for e.g. protection of the Polish national heritage. Poland's commitment to the convention will have a positive impact on our international relations and will support the Polish audiovisual sector. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u332	Thank you very much. I would like to ask Mr Piotr Adamowicz to present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u333	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I present my position on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up at Strasbourg on 8 November 2001, print No 1444. I would remind you that the Sejm, when adopting the law, agrees to the ratification of this convention by the President of the Republic of Poland. The European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage, drawn up in 2001, entered into force on 1 January 2008. Poland only signed the Convention 12 years after its entry into force in December Ub. However, it is important that, despite some delay, our national rules on the protection of audiovisual heritage are in line with European standards and to some extent even higher. The basic obligations imposed by the Convention are the introduction of a statutory obligation to collect and store cinematographic works produced in the territory of the country concerned and the identification of an institution acting as a national archive. In Poland, such a function is already performed by the National Film Library - Audiovisual Institute, hereinafter referred to as FINA. Moreover, while the convention indicates that the film producer should transmit the material within 12 months of the premiere, the Polish regulation indicates 30 days after the end of production, but no later than the day of the start of the distribution of the film. It should be stressed that neither European regulation nor national regulations allow the FINA to distribute audiovisual works commercially. The rights of creators in this area are guaranteed by the law on copyright and related rights. Ratification of the convention does not result in the need to change the provisions of Polish law, nor does it have financial implications for the budget of the state and local governments. The formal binding of the Polish Convention complements accepted international agreements. In conclusion and in relation to the current situation, I would like to refer to the recitals of the 2001 Convention. They are very important, they say: the aim of the Council of Europe is to achieve greater unity between its members, in particular in order to protect and implement the ideals and principles that constitute their common heritage, and the heritage of Europe reflects the identity and cultural diversity of its peoples. On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I declare my vote in favour of the adoption by the Sejm of the Act on the ratification of the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

First of all, I would like to comment on the questions concerning the date of ratification of the Convention, as asked by Mr Szopiński, Mr Gaża asked. The decision took place in the first half of 2020 when the Minister of Culture took action to ratify the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage. Let me remind you that this is a convention. The Council of Europe, because I had the impression that sometimes, it happens often, two institutions, the European Union and the Council of Europe are confused. This is a convention. The Council of Europe, i.e. this body, which brings together more than 20 countries than the European Union, brings together 47 countries. This is a convention of this structure, not of the European Union. Among the 47 signatory countries to this convention are 20 countries, a minority, and ratification has been carried out in 11 countries, so most of the countries of the Council of Europe have just not done so yet. We, I'm talking about the government, took up the Ub in October. a resolution on granting consent to the signing of the Convention. Permanent Polish representative to the Council of Europe Ambassador Baurski in December Ub. He signed the European Convention for the Protection of Audiovisual Heritage on behalf of Poland. Thus Poland has become the 20th signatory of the Convention, which, I repeat, has so far ratified 11 Member States. The convention has enabled the process of ratifying this multilateral international agreement to begin. In July this year Prime Minister Morawiecki signed a resolution of the Council of Ministers on the submission for ratification of this convention. The purpose of this convention is known, and the rapporteur and Member State spoke about the purpose of this convention. And you also said, rightly noting that, to a large extent, actually in its entirety, the objectives of this convention have been achieved in Polish almost since 2005, since the Act on cinema, which was adopted in April 2005, in which there is an important chapter on the collection, protection and dissemination of film collections, namely the protection of audiovisual heritage. I may be a little nasty, but since I'm glad all the groups, all the political environments today see the need for such protection... As regards the protection of audiovisual heritage, it was supported by the ruling party at that time, but also by the opposition party Law and Justice, but was not supported by the Civic Platform. I reply immediately to Mrs Navel, because there was a question about what carriers this heritage is protected on. The principle is that on the media on which the reference copy is produced is supplied to the appropriate institution, but all of this means actually digitising resources, i.e. digitisation of cinematographic resources, digitisation of audiovisual resources. There was a question about 11 countries that have already ratified it. These are: Bosnia, Croatia, France, Georgia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Monaco, Germany, Serbia, Slovakia and Hungary. We will join this group of countries after the vote on this convention today or tomorrow. There have been such very general questions by Mr Suchon, whether audiovisual heritage is also protected at local level. With us, there is a rule that for each film produced, a copy must be provided in due time after production to the appropriate institution. Then he is already protected because this institution is involved in securing and possibly digitizing this material so that it can serve the next generation. This solution is generally included in our Cinema Act. Mr Rzepa asked about this process of digitisation. It is implemented in several institutions, not only in the National Film Library - Audiovisual Institute. After all, it digitizes its very rich resources from the Documentary and Fabulous Film Works in Warsaw and the Centre for Audiovisual Technologies in Wroclaw. As far as costs are concerned, for example. The National Film Library itself - The Audiovisual Institute enjoys a regular, subjective grant, systematically paid for the functioning of this institution in the amount of PLN 30 million per year. A percentage of this money is being used to digitize our audiovisual achievements. Mr Zimoch's question about other conventions. This is the responsibility of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. I can't respond to the heat. We'll tell the MFA to send this reply in writing. To answer Mrs Senyszyn's question, I would like to say that there is no need to amend the Act, because the Act on Cinema, as we have said, primarily the Act on Cinema, has ensured that audiovisual heritage is protected. For 16 years, there have been such obligations in our law. They are even, some of you have noticed this in their speeches, going further than what the Convention contains, so we have even more security than the minimum required by the Convention. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u367	Thank you. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Law on State aid in raising children and certain other laws. The draft is included in print No 1530 and the committee's report in print No 1561. High Izbo! Introduced by the Government of Law and Justice in 2016 500+ education was a breakthrough moment in which the State reached out a helping hand to Polish families. In earlier years, there was no support mechanism that would not only improve the material situation of families, but, above all, gave them the right priority. So far, over 6.5 million children have been included in the 500+ family programme, and 156 billion PLN has been added to the families. This support contributes to improving both the financial situation and the quality of life of families. Importantly, the amendment of the law does not introduce any changes to the rights of families, their access to the benefit of 500+. The aim of the changes is only to optimise the provision process by using increasingly common tools such as IT systems, and to put emphasis on non-cash forms of payment of educational benefits, as well as to reduce the costs of granting and transferring eligible funds by relying on the Social Insurance Institution. It is envisaged to automate the law investigation and issue decisions by providing the appropriate software in the Social Security Office, which will reduce the costs of handling applications because most applications will not have to be handled by the employee. Only cases requiring analysis of the Annex to the application or investigation with the applicant will be carried out by a ZUS employee. The change in the form of applications - only by electronic means - was verified by the introduction of 1 July this year. A good start for the pilot scheme. It runs without any technical or organizational problems. Similarly as in the case of the so-called. the 300+ programme, as in the case of the 500+ programme, measures will be taken to select applications only via the Internet. The development of digitalisation allows these benefits to be automated. This translates into significant savings, which can be passed on to the operation of further family support programmes, including the draft law on family care capital that is being launched tomorrow. The amendment introduces payment of benefits only in non-cash form. For example: the cost of ordering a transfer by ZUS is only 6 gr, with the cost of ordering a single post transfer, the postal transfer is already 7 PLN 12 gr. This amendment aims at saving costs on the part of the distribution of the benefit and simplifying the process, since only in the case of less than 2% of persons, the payment of the benefit takes place in a form other than a transfer to a bank account. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I request that the draft law contained in the report of the Committee on Printing No 1561 be adopted. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u431	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I'll try to answer all the questions completely. And those repeated repeatedly, and those less repeated. I'll start over. The 500+ family programme introduced almost 6 years ago was at the beginning of the social and universal programme in the sense that the benefit was paid for each second and subsequent child. For the first child, the income criterion was applied. After a few years, the social element related to the income criterion was abolished. Today it is a universal benefit, a universal benefit, a benefit provided for every child in Poland. Therefore, it is a very simple provision in service, very simple in the field of verification of persons entitled to this benefit, because it is enough to check in the PESEL database. After a few years of experience, we pay attention to the costs of operating this system, which in today's shape of this system are invaluable. This is demonstrated by the fact that, according to the impact assessment of this regulation, today presented, the savings related to the change we are proposing are more than PLN 3 billion in the perspective of the decade. This is a serious amount for us and we believe it is worth changing the scope or way of operating this benefit without loss to the beneficiaries. I stress once again that there are no changes in the payment of transfers of these benefits to families with children. Of course, the form changes. This form has already been, you can say, worked over and some processes by digitization have been accelerated in relation to the pandemic. This is, perhaps, the only positive side of the pandemic. We have checked how this works on several different examples, such as: tourist voucher, solidarity allowance, today's 300+ programme. In none of these cases there were any technical, organisational problems preventing such programmes from being implemented in this way, so we decided as a government that we also wanted to continue with the payment of benefits 500+, which would allow us, as I said, to save very specific measures. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u433	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the governmental bill amending the law on the income of local government units and certain other laws. These are 1531 and 1576 prints. I will immediately point out that print project 1531 was the subject of the committee's work yesterday when the first reading of the project took place. He had another title at the time, namely: a draft law on the support of local government units in connection with the Polish Lad Programme. At today's meeting, the committee changed the title of this draft law, and it now has another title, which I quoted at the beginning. Ladies and gentlemen! The bill is related to the implementation of the Polish Lad program. The current system of finances of local government units has been in operation for 17 years. In connection with the tax reform, which will be carried out by the Sejm and which will leave more money in pockets about 90% society, there is also a need to secure the finances of local government units. The guidelines that set out the draft law are primarily the support of local authorities in the framework of general subsidies, namely the modernisation of the fiscal rules of local government units, which aim to make them more flexible, but at the same time to improve the sustainability of the finances of local government units by reducing the exposure of these finances to the economic economy, while ensuring the sustainable stability of the vast majority of local governments, especially the poorer ones. I want to give you an example here. He was not part of the discussion at our committee meeting, but this is a historical example of the recent past, when we were dealing with, ladies and gentlemen, the financial crisis related to the bankruptcy of Lehman Brothers in 2008. We were also dealing with the public finance crisis in Poland in 2009 and 2010. I was a self-government officer back then. As income from PIT and CIT decreased in 2009 by around 5 and 5.5% compared to 2008 depending on the different categories of local authorities, in 2010 compared to 2009 by a further 4,5% , in sum, over these 2 years, PIT and CIT tax revenues of local governments have decreased by around 10%. At that time, there was no aid response from the government to 2900 of all local governments in Poland in the municipal, district and voivodeship degrees. Ten years later, we were dealing with an outbreak of COVID. We were worried that this would be a year for public finances, including local government finances, in March and April 2020. It turned out that further months, from May after the end of the year, resulted in significant compensation for these revenues and after the RIO's report on the financial situation of local government units for 2020 we can safely conclude that PIT and CIT revenues decreased by 1% over the whole year 2020, and yet the government came out with an initiative of aid to local authorities in the form of three tranches, a total of PLN 12 billion, of which the first tranche, in the amount of half of this, i.e. PLN 6 billion, in an algorithmic form, and the remaining two tranches in the form of a competition. Therefore, such practices, which are taking place, and sudden fluctuations in growth, can cause very large perturbations in the finances of local government units. This law, through safeguards, through various security mechanisms, which I will not talk about, may be addressed in detail by the Minister, also takes this into account. This is at the same time to ensure that the investment potential of local government units is strengthened - I am about to develop this issue - and also to provide regional governments with the income necessary to carry out public tasks, as some of the existing provisions adopted in 2003 expire at the end of December 2021 and we have the opportunity to introduce permanent solutions. As for concrete solutions, this is the introduction of a income rule ensuring the long-term sustainability of local government finances. I already told you that. This is also the introduction of a completely new part to the general subsidy, namely the development part, as well as the basis of the corrective and equalisation mechanism on the current impact from PIT and CIT. Ladies and gentlemen! The Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on Public Finances, following their first reading and examination of the project, together with the amendments, request the adoption by the High Sejm of the proposed bill. I would just like to add that amendments have also been tabled and that the amendments tabled by the opposition Members will also be voted as a final vote on minority proposals. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u435	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice strongly supports the draft law under the new title of amending the law on the financing of local government units, while in fact in its original form the name of this project was more expressing the ideas and purpose of this law, namely the aid to local government units connected with the implementation of the Polish Government. Polish Load is a new tax law that will revolutionize for all citizens. I would like to remind you that it introduces a tax-free amount of 30 thousand, so it de facto introduces the lowest pensions without tax, not only the lowest, because in total up to 2,5 thousand. gross zloty, so this is the vast majority of pensions. These lowest salaries will also be subject to a very symbolic rate of PIT. By force such beneficial changes for taxpayers, for citizens, make the impact of the PIT tax a little less. However, it is worth recalling what self-government is. Self-government is a community of inhabitants, so we should remember that the richer the richer the inhabitants of a given local government are, the richer and richer the local government is. This is something that we completely forget about in this discussion, as if we were taking away the functions of self-government, the essence of self-government from the inhabitants, from meeting their needs, from what the government is really responsible for and to whom it is to serve. After discussions at committee meetings, it was felt that maybe the most important ones were local government officials - not the need for residents. The needs of residents in the Polish Łódź are secured in a way that was much better than in previous years, because Polish Ład and the support of local authorities for the needs of local residents are among others. there is not only this law that compensates for the losses in the PIT tax, but also these are investment subsidies. I will remind you that in 2020 alone there were three tranches so-called. COVID aid, of course for specific investments, but there was also a tranche of more than PLN 6 billion distributed in an algorithmic manner, thus directly supplementing the lack of funding in local governments directly to specific investments according to what the local government planned. This law also proposes to supplement the income from the share of PIT and CIT to the level of what the local government has planned, and even if we add further coefficients, it is much more. In this law there is also an extremely important provision that even if these losses were much larger than we expect, they may be deeper for some local governments, it also guarantees the level at least as in the previous year. Thus, despite the high costs of introducing the Polish Load, despite the very significant measures that will remain in taxpayers' pockets, will remain in the pockets of citizens, but also residents of a particular government, the Polish government does not leave the local government completely without any help, as it has sometimes been, different in different years, only compensates, at least to the level which the local government planned.
