ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u574	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Commission: Infrastructure and Regional Government and Regional Policy, I present a report on the government draft law amending the Construction Law and the Law on Planning and Land Planning. The draft is contained in print No 1534 and the report of the joint committees is contained in print No 1544. High Izbo! The need for changes in this project is due to the need to provide simple and functional legal solutions for building single-family residential buildings to meet their own housing needs and to building small individual recreation buildings to meet the needs of rest. High Izbo! Poland does not end in big cities. In smaller towns where developers don't work, Poles also want to live in comfortable and functional houses. And that's why the rules are here. This project introduces the possibility of building such buildings in the so-called procedure. notification with a construction project without obtaining a building permit, without setting up a construction manager and keeping a construction log. Investor for the construction of a stand-alone two-storey single-family residential building up to 70 m High Izbo! The so-called procedure. notification with a construction project is that in accordance with Article. 30a of the Act - Construction law the competent local authority of the architectural and construction administration shall publish for a period of not less than 30 days and not more than 60 days the relevant information in the Public Information Bulletin, from where interested persons may draw information about the investment undertaken. In the procedure we are talking about here, the party is only the owner of the plot under construction. The building will be subject to notification of the completion of the construction referred to in Article. 54 (1) (a) 1 law - Construction law. High Izbo! The draft in question therefore amends Article. Article 29 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Construction rights. After point 1 in that paragraph. 1 is added point 1a relating to one-family buildings with a building area of up to 70 m already discussed by me. High Izbo! In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 of this project is amended to the Law of 27 March 2003 on spatial planning and development. In the absence of a local plan, it is necessary to issue a decision on the conditions of construction, because the process of authorising the implementation of the investment is in Polish almost two stages, and the municipality has competence in the field of spatial management. It is a complicated and difficult procedure, requiring an analysis of the functions and characteristics of buildings and land development and numerous arrangements. This draft law provides for the facilitation of this decision, inter alia. clarification of the urban analysis area to three times the width of the ground front, but not less than 50 m, limitation of the parties' participation, because the party to the proceedings is the applicant. This decision for the building, i.e. the building's construction conditions for a single-family building, is to be issued within 21 days. However, this deadline does not include dates which are independent of the authority, i.e. the deadline for completing the application, the agreement to decide whether or not to carry out the environmental impact procedure if required. Nevertheless, the authority issuing the decision on the construction conditions will have to make efforts to carry out it efficiently, as a higher level of penalty may be imposed for delays in its issuance. High Izbo! Yesterday, the Joint Committees: Infrastructure and the Territorial Government and Regional Policy carried out the first reading of the draft Print Act No 1534 that I discussed here, and after its completion and after the rejection of the proposal to reject the project - and I would add that 12 Members voted against, 28 were against and 1 abstained - carried out detailed work on the project. Eight amendments to the Act, including an amendment clarifying the wording of point 1a in Article, were adopted in the course of this work. Article 29 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Construction law to eliminate interpretation doubts concerning this provision, namely whether it is possible to build such a building on a plot less than 500 m High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, please adopt the draft contained in the report of the Joint Committee in print No 1544. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u582	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! It should be acknowledged that the proposed regulation responds to social expectations in terms of providing simple legal solutions for building single-family homes in order to meet their own housing needs. The regulation introduces the possibility of building single-family houses without obtaining a building permit, setting up a construction manager and keeping a construction log, in the so-called procedure. applications with a construction project. In the absence of a local spatial planning plan, a decision on the conditions of construction for such houses shall be taken within 21 days. The subject of the application, to which the project of land or land development and the architectural and construction project will have to be attached, will be a building with a building area of up to 70 m. Any reasonable solution that makes it easier for citizens to get their own home and meet the needs of their families deserves support. That is why we perceive the proposed regulation as a step in the right direction. It is worth recalling that similar rules were introduced by the Platform-PSL coalition. However, under the spoon the question arises as to whether the design of the regulation properly secures issues related to the full safety of building delivery and spatial governance and urban aesthetics. It should be borne in mind that, when submitting documentation in this procedure, the authority, the municipality, will not even have the competence to request the completion of the documents by the investor. It will be possible to submit an incomplete application, in fact even without a construction project, which will not even be joined by chance, and start construction immediately after delivery of the documents. The Authority may not even request completion of documentation or interruption of construction. It will be possible to submit such an application even without construction conditions. This is a certain kind of puriazum and can lead to major urban chaos. We also think that it is not justified to eliminate the possibility for the municipalities to verify the documentation. It seems reasonable, however, to maintain a certain opposition when the documentation is not in accordance with the regulations, incompatible with the local plan, construction conditions or taking place, for example. building a house in the woods. Because let us remember that according to this procedure you will be able to build a house in the woods and the commune body will not be able to block it in any way. [[Speech Off the Microphone]] Yes, there's no way to block this process. [[You haven't read...]] We don't want to... We consider part of the intention of this project to be right, but the construction process cannot be carried out without any control by the authorities of the municipality. Therefore, we would like to restore the possibility for the municipality to request that the documentation be completed, to object, so that there may even be some minimum control over this process. We welcome this legislative step, which we believe is a step in the right direction. We stress, however, that the government's policy lacks a coherent approach to the overall regulation of the subject effectively increase the accessibility of citizens to housing and homes, whether for their own or for rent. Unfortunately, the actions taken so far are only scrappy and not complex. However, the parliamentary club of the Polish Coalition and PSL tabled amendments. We depend on these amendments on the outcome of the final vote, but recognize the intention of the applicants that the project actually meets part of the expectations of Poles. [[Applause]]

Denmark is one of the few European countries that have no grave of an unknown soldier. Perhaps because its status during World War II was slightly different from that of the rest of the nations occupied by Germany. There is a cemetery in Copenhagen, where the heroes of the Danish resistance sleep forever. The only Pole lying there is Lucian Butterch's peter. After graduation in 1931. Young Lucjan started his studies at the Gdynia Maritime School. He became an officer of the merchant fleet, and according to a regional researcher, Jerzy Kisson-Jaszczyński, he was often in his hometown, bringing with him stories about distant countries. In the campaign for September. Butter has crossed the battle route of the Army, fighting in the throat of the Pomeranian corridor and later in the battle of Bzura. He was taken into captivity, from which he tried to flee three times. He broke through to Denmark, where he headed a diversionary and spy network called "Felicia" at the command of the Polish Armed Forces in London. Other sources put him at the head of a secret Polish organization. Historians disagree, the subject needs further research. It is certain, however, that Lucjan Buttercha was active in the underground, both by providing essential intelligence to London and by participating in sabotage operations in Denmark. On December 31, 1944, Piotrkowian married his friend Lone Louise Christine Mogensen, born in Poland, Dunka, who also worked in the resistance movement. Unfortunately, for reasons which until today are unknown, most likely as a result of treason, at night from 2 to 3 January their hideout was surrounded by the Gestapo. Lucjan Butterch was defending himself to the last bullet, and his young wife committed suicide by chewing on an amp of poison. In Copenhagen in Gaju Remembrance in Ryvangen there is only one foreigner - a Pole piotrkowian. It's a pore. Lucjan Butters. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Orchestra Data Cement Plant Chelm has been playing for half a century. The orchestra was founded in 1971. The beginnings of the orchestra were not easy. It was formed by a group of amateur musicians who gathered in their spare time for music together. The orchestra's members were mostly factory employees and school students. Leszek Wiata became the first band's bandmaster. The approval and interest of the Chełm cement combination and the good conditions created by the company's cultural house, as well as the solid work of the entire team, led the orchestra to begin rehearsals and performances. The first performance was the first march in 1971 - the orchestra participated in these celebrations for many years. You didn't have to wait long to succeed. The orchestra performed more and more not only in Chelm itself, but also in the country. It was present on many socialist constructions carried out by Polish specialists in Czechoslovakia, the GDR or the USSR, as well as, as few know, it welcomed the returners from the sea cruise of the ship in the Gdynia port. In 1979, the orchestra was entrusted to the conductor Tadeusz Świek. The change of the bandmaster contributed to an even greater level of musical orchestra. The objectives of the competition include shaping patriotic attitudes of young people by promoting and deepening knowledge about the organization and activities of the Polish Victory Service, the Union of Armed Fighting, the Home Army, cultivating values, ideals and attitudes of soldiers of the Polish Underground State, inspiring and encouraging young people to undertake independent research on the history of their family, the environment associated with the place of residence, improving their ability to use various forms of expression, relationships, interviews, stories, reports, and finally visiting memorial sites. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u663	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Next Friday, September 17th, we celebrate the World Sybirak Day. This important celebration was established on the anniversary of the Russian attack on Poland and although the celebrations organized by the Sibirak Union have been held annually since 1991, it was only in 2013 that the Polish Sejm formally established the day of 17 September Sibirak. History has many ways of persecuting the population because of nationality, social status, or religion. Concentration camps, mass executions, or eventually alienated fundamental human rights, stripped humanity of humanity. These actions are an expression of the darkest pages of history, the result of which was not only to humiliate the victims, but also to glorify the power of the oppressors. This was the fate of the Sibirians, who were massively exported into the cold guts of the USSR and on the endless steppes of Kazakhstan - crowded in cattle cars, decimated by diseases and frost. Their bones still lie in unknown graves by roads and tracks, in forests and on broken steppe land. At this point it should be emphasized that our fellow countrymen in Siberia began in the 17th century. The first group of exiles was about 10,000. participants of the bar confederation, in the following centuries in Russia insurgents who participated in all independence breaks were sent: Kościuszko, November and January. The turn of the 19th and 20th centuries is the time of the troops of independence conspiracy activists, including General Józef Piłsudski. The blackest part of the story concerning the exiles of Poles to the Far East began with the Soviet aggression in September 1939. After September 17, over 1.5 million Poles were deported to Siberia and Kazakhstan. Sibir consumed many lives, hundreds of thousands of victims were not allowed to return to their homeland. Those who have succeeded in this are still remembering the humiliating conditions in which they live. The exile left a deep scar in the consciousness of all who came to experience it. Although many years have passed since those tragic events, the memory of the harm that has been done is still intact. Today, the last sibiracs are slowly leaving, along with them memories and stories of inhuman events that occurred to them on distant eastern earth. September 17 is a special date in the history of Poland, which is marked by the great pain and suffering of millions of Poles. Remember, this day has a unique dimension especially for sibiraks and kresas, it is a symbol of their life, and at the same time an eloquent way to preserve in the memory of their tragedies and wandering fate. Let us make every effort to ensure that the dramatic events of this period survive in the memory and consciousness of subsequent generations. Therefore, let us devote this day to meetings with former sibiracs to listen to their difficult stories and then be able to pass them on to the next generation. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-16-sejm-37-2.u665	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! As a member of the Communication Commission with Poles abroad, I wanted to introduce the characters of outstanding Poles who created and conducted important cultural and social activities for Poland abroad. I wanted to start with Mieczysław Grydzewski, who was born on 27 December 1894 in Warsaw in a Jewish family as Mieczysław Grützhändler. He started his education in the private High School of the Warsaw City Buyers' Meeting. He began his university studies in Moscow, where he studied law, then studied history and philosophy with Marcel Handelsman and Władysław Tatarkiewicz at the University of Warsaw. In 1922 he received a doctorate in Polish-French relations during the time of King Stanisław August Poniatowski. During World War I, he actively sympathized with the soccer team and served at the Press Office of the Supreme Command. He was among the founders and editors of Skamandra. During the interwar period, for the creation of the weekly Literary News, together with Anton Borman, they sold a ring after Grydzewski's mother and a set of tires to obtain share capital. This week has become a truly pluralistic medium that allowed everyone to speak, regardless of their cultural or political affiliation. They wrote there, among other things. Stanisław Żeromski and Melchior Wańkowicz. Grydzewski also published in collaboration with Julian Tuwim and Anton Borman a magazine called To-To. He was also active for La Pologne Literaire and the writings of Culture. On September 3, 1939, the last issue of the Literary News was published, and soon Grydzewski left for France. In Paris he began to publish Polish, Political and Literature news since 1940. Then he was forced to go to Great Britain, where he continued to publish and edit the weekly news until 1966. This outstanding author on emigration also led the Academy of Literature on emigration. He died in 1970. His ashes were scattered at Golders Green Cemetery.
